Fishing, woodworking, even the first-aid workshops — those usually help me clear my head, and lately, since my father-in-law moved in, I just don’t make myself go. I’m holding it together at work, but at home and with coursework I’m more wound up and short, so those things have kind of fallen off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, depressed, or hopeless lately, especially when you're at home or dealing with coursework?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it depressed or hopeless. It’s more like I get worn down and frustrated when I’m at home or trying to focus on coursework. I haven’t really thought of it as being “down,” exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your mood feels worse specifically when you’re at home or working on coursework compared to other times?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s pretty much when I notice it. At work I can keep myself together, but when I’m at home, or trying to sit down with coursework, I get a lot more wound up and short than usual. Since my father-in-law moved in, it’s just been harder to keep my head clear, and I’ve been skipping the things that usually help, like fishing, woodworking, and the first-aid workshops.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired or that you're lacking energy lately, especially when you're at home or working on coursework?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed being more tired or low on energy, no. It’s more that I feel tense or distracted in those situations, but I wouldn’t say I’m exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you felt down or lost interest in most activities, almost every day for most of the day, even if it was just for a short period?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t had that kind of all-day, almost-every-day feeling, and I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest in most things like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried about a number of different things, like work, school, home life, or other responsibilities, even when you're not in those situations?\n\nPatient: Not really across everything, no. It’s more tied to what’s going on at home since my father-in-law moved in, and when I’m trying to do coursework. I can keep it together at work, but at home I get wound up and short, and I’ve stopped doing some of the things that usually help me clear my head, like fishing or woodworking.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or having trouble relaxing even when you're not at home or working on coursework, or does this tension seem to fade when you're away from those situations?\n\nPatient: It mostly fades when I’m away from those situations. And I’ve been skipping things like fishing, woodworking, even first-aid workshops—the stuff that usually helps me clear my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself worrying or feeling anxious about going home or working on coursework even before you're actually in those situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. I’ve also been skipping the things that usually help me clear my head, like fishing and woodworking.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about things at home or with your coursework, even when you're not directly dealing with them, like when you're at work or trying to rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah, some, but it’s not like I’m worrying about everything all day. I wouldn’t say I don’t care about them anymore, but I’ve been skipping them. Fishing, woodworking, even the first-aid workshops — those usually help me clear my head, and lately, since my father-in-law moved in, I just don’t make myself go. I’m holding it together at work, but at home and with coursework I’m more wound up and short, so those things have kind of fallen off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, depressed, or hopeless lately, especially when you're at home or dealing with coursework?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it depressed or hopeless. It’s more like I get worn down and frustrated when I’m at home or trying to focus on coursework. I haven’t really thought of it as being “down,” exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your mood feels worse specifically when you’re at home or working on coursework compared to other times?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s pretty much when I notice it. At work I can keep myself together, but when I’m at home, or trying to sit down with coursework, I get a lot more wound up and short than usual. Since my father-in-law moved in, it’s just been harder to keep my head clear, and I’ve been skipping the things that usually help, like fishing, woodworking, and the first-aid workshops.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired or that you're lacking energy lately, especially when you're at home or working on coursework?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed being more tired or low on energy, no. It’s more that I feel tense or distracted in those situations, but I wouldn’t say I’m exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you felt down or lost interest in most activities, almost every day for most of the day, even if it was just for a short period?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t had that kind of all-day, almost-every-day feeling, and I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest in most things like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried about a number of different things, like work, school, home life, or other responsibilities, even when you're not in those situations?\n\nPatient: Not really across everything, no. It’s more tied to what’s going on at home since my father-in-law moved in, and when I’m trying to do coursework. I can keep it together at work, but at home I get wound up and short, and I’ve stopped doing some of the things that usually help me clear my head, like fishing or woodworking.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or having trouble relaxing even when you're not at home or working on coursework, or does this tension seem to fade when you're away from those situations?\n\nPatient: It mostly fades when I’m away from those situations. At work I can hold it together pretty well. It’s when I’m back at home, with my father-in-law living with us now, or when I’m trying to focus on coursework, that I get wound up and short. And I’ve been skipping things like fishing, woodworking, even first-aid workshops—the stuff that usually helps me clear my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself worrying or feeling anxious about going home or working on coursework even before you're actually in those situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. Even before I get home, I’ll find myself thinking about how tense things might be with my father-in-law there, or how I’m going to get through coursework later. I can still keep it together at work, but once I’m heading back into that stuff, I get wound up. I’ve also been skipping the things that usually help me clear my head, like fishing and woodworking.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about things at home or with your coursework, even when you're not directly dealing with them, like when you're at work or trying to rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah, some, but it’s not like I’m worrying about everything all day. It’s mainly the stuff at home since my father-in-law moved in, and the coursework. I can hold it together at work, but when I start thinking about going home or having to sit down and study, I get wound up. And I’ve been letting the things that usually clear my head—fishing, woodworking, those first-aid workshops—kind of drop off.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your anxiety or tension is mainly limited to situations involving your home life and coursework, and that you feel much better when you're away from those specific stressors?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s pretty much it. I’m okay most of the time at work, but since my father-in-law moved in, home feels a lot more tense. And when I’ve got coursework to deal with, I get more wound up and short than I’m used to. I’ve also been skipping the things that usually help me clear my head, like fishing, woodworking, and those first-aid workshops.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel anxious or worried about home or coursework on more days than not?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I’d say more days than not lately. It’s mostly tied to home since my father-in-law moved in, and then the coursework on top of that. I’m still doing okay at work, but when I’m home or trying to study, I get more wound up and short than usual. I’ve also been skipping the stuff that usually helps me clear my head, like fishing and woodworking.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or tense in situations that aren’t related to home or your coursework, like when you’re out with friends, during free time, or even while working on something you enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. It’s more when I’m home since my father-in-law moved in, or when I have to sit down for school, that I get wound up and short. And I’ve kind of stopped doing the things that usually help me clear my head, like fishing, woodworking, and the first-aid workshops.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that the worry or anxiety about home or your coursework feels constant or hard to control, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: It’s not constant exactly. I can hold it together at work, but once I’m home or trying to deal with coursework, I get more wound up and short than I’m used to. Since my father-in-law moved in, home just feels harder to settle in. And I’ve been skipping the things that usually clear my head, like fishing, woodworking, and the first-aid workshops, so that probably hasn’t helped.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your worry or anxiety about home or coursework happens on more days than not and feels hard to control, even when you’re not directly in those situations?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s hard to control all the time when I’m away from it. I can still hold it together at work. But since my father-in-law moved in, once I’m home or trying to do coursework, I get wound up and short pretty quickly.
